Charts of the week: No capitulation

A hotter-than-expected US inflation report triggered a pullback in risk assets, but there are few signs of capitulation so far 
US core and supercore inflation; US inflation breadth*    Our ‘Fear and Greed’ indicator

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *share of US consumer inflation components rising more than 3%

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary:  Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid -term elections. The near -term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive , the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike:  Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm . Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far.  Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all -time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 1 8.2, still well 
under the 25 –30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September.  Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negativ e (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks?  We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25 –30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index  enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid -term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings:  
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable . We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike:  The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10 -year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near -term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10 -year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while  the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation  came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish . Unsurprisingly, the market -
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes  by June 
2027. A December rate hike  now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the  US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would  
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre -existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are your key takeaways from US bank earnings? 
Our view: Q3 2026 earnings results from major US banks have 
been mixed, but underlying trends remain encouraging. Healthy 
loan and deposit growth, resilient consumer spending and 
wealth management activity continue to support the sector. 
Credit risks remain manageable. We remain constructive on the 
US financial sector, favouring stronger, diversified franchises. 
Rationale: Healthy loan and deposit growth continue to support net 
interest income (NII), hinting that the US economy remains resilient. 
Capital markets revenue grew year -on-year (y/y) , but moderated 
sequentially after a very strong Q2 2026, although performance 
varied across banks. Management commentary points to a healthy 
deal pipeline , supported by M&A activity and debt refinancing 
demand, although execution will depend on market conditions. 
Provisioning and net charge -off trends were mixed across banks, 
with no clear signs of broad deterioration in credit quality. Firmer 
US inflation raises the risk of further monetary tightening. While 
higher rates can support asset yields, they can also increase 
funding costs, pressure borrowers and weigh on securities ’ 
valuations. We will continue to monitor lending demand, credit 
costs, NII trends, capital markets momentum and capital returns.  
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Can Big Tech’s earnings guidance sustain confidence in 
the AI investment cycle? 
Our view: We expect Big Tech’s AI spending to remain elevated, 
supporting our constructive AI capex outlook. However, we 
await guidance on the pace of capex growth and the 
sustainability of profit margins. 
Rationale: Semiconductor leaders’ latest company guidance points 
to supply constraints extending through 2028, supporting continued 
capacity expansion for compute . However, while AI capex is still 
likely to rise, y/y growth is expected to moderate  from c.100% in 
Q3 2026 to c.20% in Q3 2027. This normalisation in growth could 
raise concerns about the AI investment cycle’s durability.  
The Nasdaq-100 Index has rallied alongside accelerating AI capex. 
Slower capex growth is likely to moderate semiconductor revenue 
growth, while hyperscalers continue to incur depreciation costs on 
past investments. The slower growth is already partially reflected in 
the Nasdaq -100’s valuation de -rating this year. While we remain 
constructive on the AI investment theme, we prefer to stay 
prudent in the near term and await Big Tech’s guidance for greater 
visibility into spending plans in H2 2027 and beyond. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would France face a credit rating downgrade by rating 
agencies this month? What might be the market implications? 
Our view: A rating downgrade is unlikely unless France’s 2027 
budget passes the National Assembly very soon with material 
changes to the proposal. We see French government bond 
yields remaining elevated relative to German Bund yields. 
Rationale: The French National Assembly began debating France’s 
2027 draft budget on 13 October 2026, with the first vote scheduled 
for 20 October. Near-term budget approval appears unlikely, as 
opposition parties have tabled motions to reject the proposal. The 
French Parliament has up to 70 days from the budget’s submission 
to complete the legislative process, although  its final adoption and 
the resolution of political challenges could extend into early 2027.  
Last October, two of the three major rating agencies downgraded 
France by one notch to the high-single-A category  with stable 
outlooks. The remaining agenc y rated France in the low-AA 
category with a negative outlook. As negative outlooks are typically 
resolved within 12 -18 months, agenc ies have time to await fiscal 
clarity before acting. We believe downgrade risks are largely priced 
in after the recent rise in yields, but volatility is likely to persist amid 
fiscal uncertainty and the approaching presidential election. Banks 
and domestically-focused issuers would face the greatest near-term 
pressure. However, we would view wider spreads as an opportunity 
to add exposure, particularly to French banks’ subordinated 
debt, given their stronger balance sheets and the fact that France 
contributes less than 50% of major French banks’ overall revenue. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Japanese banks? 
Our view: We are positive on Japanese banks despite the recent 
market correction, as the structural drivers of improving 
profitability and shareholder returns remain intact . While 
political risk and shifting BoJ rate hike odds could sustain near-
term volatility, we see the recent pullback as an opportunity to 
build selective exposure to Japanese bank stocks. 
Rationale: Japan’s gradual monetary policy normalisation  
should support banks’ NII as floating-rate loans reprice and maturing 
assets are reinvested at higher yields. Healthy corporate lending 
demand, improving capital efficiency and continued share buybacks 
provide further support . While uncertainty over the pace of BoJ 
tightening could weigh on sentiment, we see gradual interest rate 
normalisation as supportive of medium-term bank profitability.  
Key risks  include rising deposit costs, mark -to-market losses on 
bond portfolios and a disorderly increase in Japanese government 
bond yields. We continue to favour well-capitalised banks  with 
strong domestic deposit franchises and improving returns on equity. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Could NZD/USD rebound from its one-year trough? If so, 
how far could it recover?  
Our view: New Zealand’s improving domestic conditions, 
persistent inflation and technical support provide a basis for 
NZD/USD’s initial recovery towards the 0.57 level, although 
elevated US bond yields could slow its progress. 
Rationale: In Q3, New Zealand’s  seasonally-adjusted business 
confidence reading rose to a net 40% of firms expecting the economy 
to improve, up from net 14% in Q2 . Hiring intentions improved, and 
filled jobs rose 0.2% m/m in August. These developments suggest 
domestic conditions remain supportive for the NZD.  
New Zealand’s above-target inflation keeps further RBNZ tightening 
in focus. The 22 October consumer inflation data release and the 
28 October RBNZ policy meeting are key catalysts: persistent price 
pressures a nd improving demand are expected to prompt firmer 
policy guidance and strengthen the NZD’s interest-rate appeal. 
NZD/USD’s position near the lower boundary of its downtrend 
channel suggests scope for a technical rebound. We see 0.57 as 
the first recovery hurdle; a sustained break  above this level  would 
strengthen the case for further gains. Firmer US  consumer inflation 
remains a headwind, making a gradual recovery more plausible 
than a sharp reversal.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Can the proposed Russia -Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire ’ 
contain oil prices as Middle East risks rise? 
Our view: We expect escalating Middle East tensions to keep oil 
prices elevated . We  maintain our  three- and 12 -month West 
Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil price forecasts at USD 100/bbl and 
USD 80/bbl, respectively, and continue to favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a portfolio hedge 
against persistent inflation risks. 
Rationale: The proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ still 
looks some way off. In our view, even if an agreement is reached, it 
would do little to ease the supply risks building in the Middle East. 
Gulf oil exports have recovered to above 80% of pre-conflict levels 
in recent weeks, helping moderate the pace of price increases. 
However, the recovery remains vulnerable to disruptions, particularly 
following the latest Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure . 
Diesel and jet fuel remain under the most pressure , with Gulf 
exports of these fuels in September  at only around 60% of pre -
conflict levels. A sustained disruption to major Saudi production or 
export infrastructure remains the principal upside risk to oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

## Document 476: Westpac Wave - Fiji Quarterly Economic Update (2026-11-03)

WESTPAC ECONOMICS

FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE AND OUTLOOK

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
Fiji’s resilience is being tested...
Fiji’s economy entered 2026 with growth already normalising
from the post-pandemic recovery. Real GDP growth slowed from
3.6% in 2024 to 2.5% in 2025. Foreign reserves started the year

around $3.7bn, banking-system liquidity remained high and the
RBF maintained its Overnight Policy Rate at 0.25%.
Activity so far into 2026 has been somewhat stronger than what
we feared. Visitor arrivals reached 762,352 during January–
September, up 3.7%yr, while construction, investment lending,
cement sales, remittances and private-sector credit remained
firm. Nevertheless, Westpac Fiji has kept its 2026 growth
forecast unchanged at 2.0%. Stronger headline indicators are

not translating one-for-one into real economic growth because
higher prices and intermediate costs are absorbing a growing
share of nominal activity.
The outlook changed materially in March as the Middle East
conflict disrupted global energy markets. Fiji remains heavily
dependent on imported petroleum products, so higher refined-
fuel, freight and insurance costs flowed through transport,
electricity, food distribution and wider business expenses. By

June, motor spirit price reached $3.93/litre and diesel at a record
$4.58/litre, while automotive-fuel sales value rose 24.4%yr in the
June quarter.
As this report goes out, the Middle East conflict remains a
key external downside risk. At least 12 vessels were attacked
in the week ending 16 October and yet it is reported that oil
flows through Hormuz averaged 11.6mn barrels per day to 13
October, about 68% of pre-war levels through alternative and

costly routes. Iran says a seven-day reopening proposal remains
available, but US-Iran negotiations remain unresolved.
Westpac Economics’ most recent scenario analysis “ Persistent
energy pressures have further to run ” indicates that relief will
be gradual. Brent crude is forecast to average US$101/bbl in Q4
2026 before easing through 2027. Refined products are expected
to normalise more slowly because refinery capacity, inventories,
freight and insurance remain constrained. Under Westpac’s

escalation scenario, in a return to open conflict Brent could reach
around US$150/bbl in H1 2027.
Domestic tourism sector demonstrates the nominal-versus-
real growth tension. June-quarter visitor arrivals increased to
287,588, yet earnings fell 4.9%yr to $708.1mn. First-half tourism
earnings declined 1.7%yr despite higher arrivals, even though
hotel takings increased 9.5%yr.
Australia and New Zealand remain critical, together contributing
around 71% of September arrivals. Westpac expects Australian

growth to slow from 2.1% in 2026 to 1.5% in 2027, while New
Zealand strengthens from 1.9% to 2.6%. Tourism therefore
increasingly needs to focus on yield rather than visitor numbers
alone.
Shamal Chand
Senior Economist, Westpac Pacific Economics

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
...but the buffers are still doing the heavy lifting...
There are good reasons why growth held up despite these shocks.
Construction work put in place reached $155.9mn in the June
quarter, up 8.6%yr, while investment lending, cement sales and

construction-related imports subsequently remained strong.
Industrial production increased 1.5%yr, although performance
remained uneven across industries.
Inward personal remittances have been particularly impressive,
reaching $1.2bn during January–August, up 32.7%yr. We expect
remittances to reach $1.55bn this year. These inflows, with strong
diaspora support, are helping households absorb higher living
costs while simultaneously supporting Fiji’s external position.

However, the external accounts show the cost of this resilience.
Fiji’s merchandise trade deficit widened to $2.9bn in the first half,
with imports reaching $4.3bn against exports of $1.4bn. Mineral-
fuel imports alone reached around $1.2bn, up 57%yr. Foreign
reserves nevertheless recovered to approximately $4.09bn by
end-September, equivalent to 5.7 months of retained imports , also
supported by Government’s external loan draw-down.
Our in-house import-demand indicator is signalling softer

domestic momentum. Import activity over the three months
to September was down 21.3%yr and fell 39.8% compared
with the preceding three months. The broader six-month
measure declined at a more moderate 16.1% annualised pace.
While volatile, the trend adds to our view that higher prices are
weighing on real activity, despite continued strength across
tourism, remittances, credit and construction.
Headline inflation reached 7.6%yr in August before easing to

6.8% in September, with transport prices up 12.2%yr and food
prices up 7.6%. Our latest forecast has November inflation
around 8.0%yr, average inflation around 4.3% for 2026 and
December (year-end) inflation at 7.4%.
Higher fuel prices are no longer affecting only households.
The shock is moving through freight, electricity, agriculture,
construction materials and wider supply chains. For instance,
we note recent increases in taxi and bus fares. These higher

intermediate costs compress business margins and reduce
household purchasing power.
This also informs our 2027 view. Westpac Fiji has downgraded
its 2027 growth forecast to 2.5%, reflecting the prevailing
effects of the energy crisis, elevated refined-fuel prices and the
broader transmission of higher intermediate costs into next year.
Westpac’s global outlook supports this caution: crude prices are
expected to ease gradually, but refined products could remain

comparatively expensive for longer.
Renewable energy is increasingly important because the fuel
crisis has put a clear economic price on Fiji’s imported-energy
dependence. The faster Fiji prioritises a shift to renewable energy,
the sooner it can establish a more stable energy foundation.
El Niño creates an additional risk through agriculture, hydro
generation and food prices. The buffers remain meaningful, but
they are being asked to work significantly harder.

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
FIJI ECONOMIC UPDATE
...as risks converge
Government finances add another dimension to the outlook.
The FY2025/26 deficit came in at $597.9mn, or 4.3% of GDP,
substantially better than originally budgeted.
Nevertheless, financing requirements are substantial.

Government plans to raise $1.48bn in FY2026/27, comprising
$1.046bn in deficit financing and $436.3mn refinancing. Around
$853.9mn to be domestically sourced and $627.95mn externally,
including a $444mn ADB policy-based loan in November.
Domestic yields therefore deserve closer attention. Fiji’s
government yield curve has steepened considerably, with the
adjustment concentrated beyond 10 years. While the 10-year
yield remained at 3.90%, the 20-year yield rose by 80 basis points

between July 2025 and September 2026 to 5.80%. The 25-year
yield increased even more sharply, climbing from 5.94% to 7.19%
after peaking at 7.25% in mid-2026.
Pressure is also emerging at the short end. The Government’s
20 October Treasury Bill tender offered $50mn across three-,
six- and 12-month maturities. Investors bid for the full amount,
but sought yields of 0.35%, 0.60% and 1.30%, respectively,
above the listed yields of 0.30%, 0.55% and 1.25%. Government

did not accept the higher yields, leaving the entire $50mn offer
unallocated. This suggests investors are increasingly seeking
better returns even on shorter-dated Government securities.
The financial system still provides considerable protection.
Banking-system liquidity stood around $2.28bn at end-
September and the RBF maintained its OPR at 0.25%. In August,
the weighted-average lending rate was 4.51%, the savings-
deposit rate 0.31% and the time-deposit rate 2.01%.

For now, high liquidity limits immediate crowding-out risks.
Higher government yields may encourage banks and institutional
investors to allocate more funds towards government securities.
If liquidity subsequently tightens, deposit rates may need to rise
to retain savings, adding pressure to bank funding costs and
eventually lending rates.
S&P’s decision to maintain Fiji’s B+/B ratings but revise the
outlook from stable to negative reinforces the importance of

debt sustainability, investor confidence and credible medium-
term fiscal consolidation.
Political uncertainty adds another layer, with ongoing public
debate on constitutional amendments and the general election
due by 6 February 2027.
This brings us back to the growth story. We have maintained
2026 growth at 2.0% because resilient tourism, remittances,
construction and investment are being offset by unexpectedly
high inflation and intermediate costs. Our downgrade of 2027

growth to 2.5% recognises that this adjustment will take longer
than previously expected, while fiscal, financial, energy, climate
and political risks increasingly overlap.

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
INFLATION OUTLOOK
Cost pressures move beyond the pump
Inflation has emerged as a key pressure point for Fiji, threatening 
to erode gains from otherwise resilient domestic activity. 
Headline inflation accelerated to 6.8%yr in September, driven by 
essentials: food prices rose 7.6%yr, transport increased 12.2%yr 
and housing and utilities were 4.9% higher. The underlying 
picture remains less severe, with prices declining 0.4%mth in 
September and annual-average inflation at 1.9%. Nevertheless, 
the acceleration suggests the global fuel shock is increasingly 
working its way through the domestic economy.

The import-price pipeline reinforces this view. Import prices 
increased 4.9%qtr and 4.4%yr in the June quarter, including 
a 15.4%yr rise in mineral-product prices as petrol, diesel and 
aviation-fuel costs increased. Export prices rose 6.2%yr, although 
the terms of trade eased 0.6%qtr. Elevated energy costs are 
also spreading beyond the pump. Transport-services producer 
prices increased 2.7%qtr in June, while building-material prices 
rose 1.5%qtr in September, signalling broader pressure across 
distribution, construction and services.

Fuel-price pressures intensified again in October, when regulated 
unleaded prices in Viti Levu increased to $3.15/litre and diesel 
rose by 21 cents to $3.41/litre. Prices nevertheless remain below 
their June peaks. Our indicative estimates point to further 
increases in fuel prices over the coming months.

The global outlook offers little immediate relief. Westpac 
Economics expects Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 2026 
before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027, but refined fuels are 
expected to ease more slowly as refinery supply remains more 
constrained, while shipping, insurance and inventory pressures 
gradually normalise. Westpac’s Australian forecasts provide 
some regional context, with diesel and petrol expected to 
average 274c/l and 230c/l, respectively, in Q4 before declining 
through 2027.

For Fiji, the distinction between crude and refined-product prices 
is important. Singapore gasoil prices, freight and insurance costs 
are expected to remain elevated and ease only gradually through 
2027, meaning lower Brent prices may not translate immediately 
into cheaper imported fuel. Higher diesel costs feed into freight, 
construction, agriculture and distribution, while aviation-
fuel pressures add to airline operating costs and tourism-
sector margins. Repeated shocks increase the likelihood that 
businesses eventually pass more of these costs on to consumers, 
particularly through food and other essentials.

We consequently expect inflation to remain elevated through 
year-end, rising towards 8.0% in November before easing to 7.4% 
in December, taking annual-average inflation to around 4.3%. 
The concern is increasingly that an imported fuel shock develops 
into broader cost-push inflation into next year. The squeeze on 
real purchasing power makes inflation an increasingly important 
downside risk to Fiji’s growth outlook heading into 2027.
Source: FBoS, Macrobond, Westpac Economics

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
CREDIT RATING OUTLOOK
S&P negative outlook is concerning
Fiji’s sovereign credit profile has come under renewed pressure
recently, adding another layer of caution to an economy already
navigating softer real growth and elevated external risks.

S&P maintained Fiji’s long-term rating at B+ and short-term
rating at B but revised the outlook from Stable to Negative.
While not a downgrade, it signals increased risk if fiscal
and debt metrics deteriorate. Historically, four of Fiji’s six
negative outlooks since 2006 have resulted in downgrades,
typically during periods of major economic or fiscal stress.
Similar vulnerabilities are resurfacing, with rising government
expenditure and guarantees, alongside Middle East tensions and

inflationary pressures.
The key concern is fiscal. S&P viewed the 2026/27 Budget as
more expansionary than previously expected, effectively slowing
the fiscal consolidation achieved in previous years. It projects
the fiscal deficit to average 4.6% of GDP over 2027–29, including
4.9% in FY2027, with net government debt moving above 70% of
GDP. Once contingent liabilities are incorporated, the broader
public-sector exposure approaches 80% of GDP. This leaves

considerably less fiscal space to absorb another major domestic
or external shock.
In S&P’s latest assessment, contingent liabilities are becoming
increasingly important to the sovereign risk story. Government
exposure through guarantees and other contingent liabilities
adds to the broader fiscal risk, while a widening current account
deficit, elevated imports and rising external debt reinforce the
risks surrounding the outlook. For Fiji, this matters because

the next phase of growth will require substantial investment in
infrastructure, climate resilience and productive capacity.
The negative outlook therefore strengthens the case for a more
credible medium-term fiscal consolidation path by the Fiji
Government. S&P indicated that the outlook could return to
Stable if economic growth and tourism outperform its forecasts,
resulting in narrower fiscal deficits and lower debt ratios, or if
improvements in institutional and policy settings sustainably

strengthen fiscal outcomes and debt levels.
Importantly, the assessment across rating agencies is not
uniformly negative. Moody’s maintained Fiji’s B1 long-term issuer
rating with a Stable outlook, suggesting that despite significant
structural and fiscal challenges, the overall credit profile remains
broadly unchanged and risks remain balanced.
A future downgrade would likely reinforce perceptions of
higher sovereign risk just as Fiji requires greater private and

foreign investment. Maintaining fiscal credibility, containing
contingent liabilities and ensuring that new borrowing supports
productivity-enhancing investment will be increasingly
important. With elevated uncertainty, climate vulnerability and
external shocks remaining additional downside risks, the room
for further fiscal slippage is becoming increasingly limited for the
Fijian Government.

8
WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
SUPER EL NIÑO
The threat of a “very strong” El Niño
It is the year of the ‘very strong’ El Niño, and for Fiji the risk is no
longer just on the horizon. Dry conditions are already affecting
parts of the country, while the Government has stepped up

preparations for a potentially more difficult period ahead.
History suggests this matters for the broader economy because
prolonged drought can simultaneously affect agriculture, water
supply and electricity generation before flowing through food
prices, imports, rural incomes and ultimately growth.
Our rainfall analysis covering 1981–2025 shows that rainfall
during El Niño periods was 24.7% lower than under neutral
conditions nationally, with all four regions recording historical

deficits above 20%.
During the 1982–83 El Niño, severe drought coincided with
Cyclone Oscar and a weak global economy. Historical estimates
highlight that Fiji’s economy contracted by 4.0%, against a
pre-disaster forecast of 2.7% growth during the super El Niño
period then. The 1997–98 episode was more severe. By 1998 the
cane crop was forecast at just 1.88mn tonnes, producing around
150,000 tonnes of sugar. Historical estimates used here indicate

cane production fell around 26%, contributing to an aggregate
GDP contraction of at least 1.3%. Fiji also devalued the dollar by
20% in January 1998 amid the overlapping Asian Financial Crisis.
The social cost was significant. In 1998, around 27,562
households needed food relief and 47,592 were already receiving
emergency water supplies according to some surveys. The 2015–
16 episode again brought crop losses and drying water supplies.
The effects are beginning to show

The current event is already leaving a footprint. September
rainfall was drier than usual across the Western, Central and
Eastern divisions and most of the Northern Division, while several
Western Division stations recorded less than 100mm.
More recent monitoring points to serious or very dry conditions
across parts of Vanua Levu, Taveuni, Lomaiviti, Yasawa, Lau and
other areas. Local effects are becoming tangible. Taveuni had
already required assistance with water carting in September,

while NDRMO reported that Fiji was experiencing meteorological
drought and warned it could progress towards hydrological and
agricultural drought if conditions continued.
The Government’s response has consequently shifted towards
preparedness. The National Disaster Risk Management Council
is reviewing the National El Niño Preparedness and Response
Plan. Humanitarian clusters were also instructed to prepare
contingency plans for consolidation and presentation to Cabinet,

while NDRMO has been working with Energy Fiji Limited and the
Water Authority of Fiji to assess emerging risks.
This marks a progression from earlier in the year. In August,
our assessment was that the FY2026–27 Budget contained
no explicit El Niño preparedness measure, implying much
of the initial response would need to occur through existing
institutional arrangements.
The economic transmission remains important. Agriculture feels
reduced rainfall first through weaker soil moisture and water

availability. Lower domestic food production can then lift prices
and import requirements. Electricity provides another channel
as weaker hydro generation could require increased thermal
generation and imported fuel, adding to higher production
costs and foreign-exchange outflows. And Fiji has less room for
another supply-side shock.
Fiji is more diversified than during the droughts of the 1980s
and 1990s, while its reserve position provides an important

buffer. But financial buffers cannot replace rainfall, agricultural
production or water supplies.

WESTPAC PACIFIC ECONOMICS
RESEARCH INSIGHTS
Why is ‘foreign reserves’ so important for Fiji?
Foreign reserves remain one of the most closely watched indica -
tors of Fiji’s economic health. At the end of September, reserves 
stood at around $4.1 billion, equivalent to 5.7 months of retained 
imports. The figure features prominently in Reserve Bank report -
ing, and for good reason. 
For a small, import-dependent economy operating a managed 
exchange-rate regime, reserves provide an important buffer 
against external shocks.
In simple terms, foreign reserves can be thought of as Fiji’s 
foreign-currency savings account. Fiji earns foreign exchange 
through tourism, remittances, exports, foreign investment and 
external borrowing. At the same time, foreign currency is required 
to pay for imports, service external debt and meet other overseas 
obligations. When foreign-currency inflows exceed outflows, 
reserves generally rise. When the reverse occurs, reserves come 
under pressure.

The analogy is not perfect, but it highlights why reserves matter. 
Unlike some larger economies, Fiji cannot simply rely on its ex -
change rate to absorb external shocks. The Fiji dollar is managed 
against a basket comprising the Australian, US and New Zealand 
dollars, the euro and the Japanese yen. This provides a degree 
of exchange-rate stability and predictability for households and 
businesses, but it also means that foreign reserves are needed to 
effectively provide a shock absorber for the economy.

There is also an important connection with domestic financial 
conditions. Our analysis suggests that before July 2020, every 
$1 million increase in reserves was associated with around $0.31 
million in additional banking-system liquidity. Since then, the 
relationship has strengthened substantially to around $0.83 mil -
lion. Following the pandemic, external borrowing, grants, remit -
tances and the recovery in tourism helped lift foreign-exchange 
inflows and thus banking liquidity, while government expenditure 
and limited sterilisation further contributed to abundant domes -
tic liquidity.

This highlights an important policy trade-off. Economists refer to 
the “impossible trinity”, where an economy cannot simultaneous -
ly maintain a fixed or tightly managed exchange rate, completely 
free capital movement and fully independent monetary policy. 
Fiji’s framework therefore relies on a combination of foreign re -
serves, monetary-policy settings, exchange controls and broader 
market management to maintain stability.

Those controls, however, are not without cost. Restrictions on 
moving capital offshore limit the flexibility available to house -
holds, foreign investors and Fiji-based businesses. 
A deeper question is therefore whether Fiji could eventually move 
towards a more flexible exchange-rate framework.

Probably, but not yet. Greater exchange-rate flexibility could 
theoretically allow the currency itself to absorb more of an 
external shock. But that requires considerably deeper financial 
markets than what Fiji presently has. Foreign-exchange trading 
remains relatively shallow, secondary-market activity in govern -
ment securities is limited, while instruments that allow business -
es to hedge currency risk are significantly underdeveloped.

There is also an important transmission issue. A weaker Fiji dollar 
could theoretically improve export competitiveness, but the 
benefits would take time. Merchandise exports remain con -
strained by domestic production capacity, while tourism is also 
influenced by available capacity and advance bookings. Higher 
import prices, by contrast, would flow through much faster. Given 
Fiji’s heavy reliance on imported fuel, food, machinery and other 
goods, currency depreciation would quickly add to inflation and 
business costs.

The experiences of Australia and Singapore offer two useful per -
spectives.
 Australia floated its dollar in 1983 alongside broader finan -
cial-sector liberalisation and market development. Singapore 
followed a different path. Despite possessing a highly developed 
financial system, it continues to manage its currency against a 
basket, with the exchange rate occupying a central role in mone -
tary policy.

For Fiji, the lesson is not that one exchange-rate regime is nec -
essarily superior to another. Rather, greater flexibility requires 
deeper markets and stronger transmission mechanisms. Devel -
oping the foreign-exchange market, domestic capital markets 
and currency-risk management instruments therefore needs to 
precede any significant shift in the present regime.

Until then, foreign reserves will remain much more than another 
number in the RBF’s monthly statistics. It acts as a buffer against 
external shocks, help underpin confidence in the Fiji dollar and 
provide the economy with room to continue paying for essential 
imports when foreign-currency inflows weaken. At $4.1 billion, 
the reserve position remains comfortable, but maintaining that 
buffer remains fundamental to Fiji’s macroeconomic stability .

## Document 485: Why China resists rebalancing its growth model_ (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Why China resists rebalancing its growth model_ (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026EDITORIAL
WHY CHINA RESISTS REBALANCING ITS GROWTH MODEL?
China has never exported as much as it does now, and its trade surplus has never been higher. At the same time, its
economic growth is slowing, held back by weak domestic demand. The imbalances in China’s growth model, and the ex-
ternal imbalances they give rise to, are therefore continuing to worsen. With Europe taking a tougher line and the United

States, with which China shares nothing more than ‘strategic stability’, the standoff looks set to continue. However, the
European Union and China would have a great deal to lose from a lasting deterioration in their relationship. It is in the
interests of both parties to seek to restore the balance. To this end, it is important to understand Beijing’s objectives and
the constraints facing China. Why does the country resist adjusting its growth model? Whilst exports are a vital asset for

sustaining economic activity and asserting China’s technological and industrial power, this strategy also comes at a cost.
IMBALANCES ARE WORSENING
A K-shaped economy. China’s economic growth slowed to 4.6% year-
on-year (y/y) over the first three quarters of 2026, from 5.0% in 2025.
More worryingly, the gap is widening between weak domestic demand
and the very strong performance of exports, driven by artificial intelli-
gence equipment and green technologies. Furthermore, the dynamism

of export-oriented industries has not, so far, had any positive knock-on
effects on other sectors or on households.
Domestic demand is struggling. The property crisis, now in its sixth
year, and a sluggish labour market are weighing on household confi -
dence and consumption. Since the start of 2026, households have been
prioritising debt reduction (bank loans to households have fallen by
1% y/y), house sales have continued to fall, and retail sales have been

stagnating (Chart 1). On the investment front, the decline is becoming
more pronounced and widespread: it is affecting not only the property
sector but also infrastructure and many manufacturing sectors (with
the exception of the textile industry, transport and communications,
and electronic and electrical equipment).
Exports remain very buoyant. Exports continue to be the main driver of
growth (their net contribution is expected to account for around a third

of real GDP growth in 2026, as it did in 2024–2025). Against a backdrop
of rebound in global trade, Chinese companies maintained their market
share at 14.6% of total exports (measured in US dollars) in the first
half of 2026, as they had in 2025. Growth in goods exports accelerated
(21.9% y/y in USD over the first nine months, compared with 5.2% in
2025), driven by both volumes and prices. These have rebounded after
three years of decline, driven by rising costs of raw materials and the

prices of goods required for AI, but they have increased at a slower rate
than the global price average. Exports from high-tech sectors (batte -
ries, electric vehicles, robots and AI equipment) are posting the highest
growth rates (+55.8% y/y for all AI-related goods over the first nine
months of 2026).
Record external surpluses. Imports, which had been stagnating since
2022, have been recovering since the start of the year (up 27.2% y/y
over the first nine months), also driven by both volumes and prices.

The countries benefiting from this rebound in the Chinese market are
commodity exporters and Asian producers integrated into AI value
chains (South Korea in particular). Otherwise, China has significantly
reduced its reliance on imports over the past few years. Overall, the
trade surplus has grown at a slower rate in 2026 than in previous years.
Nevertheless, it has still reached a record high: USD1,249 billion over
the twelve months to September 2026, which is three times its 2019

level (see Chart 2).
SOURCE: CPB, NBS, BNP PARIBAS

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Meanwhile, the current account surplus is expected to approach a re -
cord level of USD 800 billion in 2026, representing nearly 4% of China’s
GDP (a ratio already seen in the late 2000s). As a proportion of global
GDP , this is unprecedented (at 0.6%).
Imbalances set to persist. The imbalances inherent in the growth mo-
del are likely to persist, fuelled notably by the strong price and non-

price competitiveness of Chinese goods. This competitiveness remains
underpinned by: i/ innovation efforts and the move up the value chain
in high-tech sectors and the low-carbon transition; ii/ low production
costs (labour, low-cost credit, land and energy, tax benefits 1); iii/ the
yuan’s exchange rate. Its slow appreciation since mid-2025 has not
offset the cumulative depreciation from 2022 to 2025 (nominal and
real depreciation due to low inflation), and its real effective exchange

rate (REER) remains 10% below its 2021 average. At the end of 2025,
the IMF estimated that the REER was undervalued by between 12%
and 20%.
Beijing’s economic strategy is exacerbating imbalances. The 15th Five-
Year Plan2, launched this year, prioritises innovation, high-tech (ex -
port-oriented) sectors and technological self-reliance above all else,
whilst reforms to the social security system and private consumption
remain on the back burner (see Chart 3). Furthermore, fiscal policy,

which had provided moderate support for household spending for a
time in 2025, was tightened in 2026 (until the recent announcements
of new measures for the final quarter).
WHY BEIJING IS DIGGING ITS HEELS IN
Beijing is not giving ground on any key issues. Faced with trading
partners calling on China to rebalance its economic model, reduce in -
dustrial subsidies and strengthen the yuan, Beijing responds that the
trade surplus reflects the competitiveness of its goods, points to the

United States’ insufficient savings, and uses critical materials as a bar-
gaining chip in negotiations.
The need for growth. Exports are a key driver of growth at a time when
the Chinese economy is undergoing a period of transition. The country
has moved away from its pre-2022 model, which was based on in -
vestment in property and traditional industries and financed by debt.
Growth is now driven by ‘new productive forces’, and exports must pro-

vide producers with the market that domestic demand does not. Fur -
thermore, innovation and the roll-out of AI must stimulate productivity
gains and offset the impact of the shrinking labour force on economic
growth. It is essential to limit the slowdown of growth in order to sup-
port job creation and absorb the economy’s excess debt.
The strategic argument. Exports are a means of asserting China’s in -
dustrial and technological supremacy, weakening its competitors, and

strengthening ‘national security’. They place China at the heart of va -
lue chains in the most strategic industries, such as AI and the low-car-
bon transition. Furthermore, the pursuit of self-sufficiency and import
substitution — which exacerbate external imbalances — also help to
build national sovereignty by reducing dependencies.
A political and fiscal choice. Expanding the social safety net is seen as
a solution for providing long-term support for household consumption3

by reducing their savings rate (estimated at 35.7% of disposable income
in 2025, which is more than double the average rate for the euro area).
1 According to the IMF, the cost of China’s industrial policy amounts to about 4% of GDP . See IMF, ‘Industrial Policy in China: Quantification and Impact on Misalloca-
tion’, August 2025: https://doi.org/10.5089/9798229017855.001
2 See BNP Paribas, EcoWeek, China’s 15th Five-Year Plan: prioritising power over addressing imbalances

3 The IMF estimates, for example, that doubling social expenditure (health, education, social services and basic pensions) in rural areas could reduce savings and
raise the ratio of private consumption to GDP by 1.8 pp in the medium term, at an annual fiscal cost of 1% of GDP . See IMF, People’s Republic of China 2025 Article IV,
February 2026: https://doi.org/10.5089/9798229038911.002
However, this is not a priority for Beijing, certainly due to ‘ideologi -

cal’ mistrust of the welfare state and a desire for fiscal prudence.
A major reform of the healthcare or pension systems would place a
strain on public finances in both the short and long term, particularly
as the population ages. Whilst the central government remains in a
sound financial position, local governments are already under strain
following several years of falling land sales proceeds, high deficits and
rising debt. The authorities do not have a large fiscal room for ma -

noeuvre and wish to preserve it in anticipation of future shocks.
LIMITATIONS AND POSSIBLE ADJUSTMENTS
In China, a society gripped by anxiety… Economic imbalances are ta -
king their toll on households. The emphasis placed on manufacturing
rather than services is having a negative impact on employment and
wages (the share of value added allocated to employees’ pay is esti -
mated at 50% in the services sector and 41% in manufacturing). The
downturn in the property market is eroding households’ wealth, and

their savings are mainly held in low-yield bank accounts. Finally, social
spending is rising slowly (by 5.2% a year since 2020, compared with
9.7% over the previous five years).
… and enterprises under pressure. The low prices of Chinese goods,
both for export and on the domestic market — resulting from excess
production capacity and ‘involution’ — are eroding corporate profit
margins. Total profits for industrial enterprises have recovered this
year but they are highly concentrated, particularly in AI-related sec -

tors, while the proportion of industrial companies reporting losses has
continued to rise (30%, compared with 26% in 2022). These vulnerabili-
ties are having increasing contagion effects on the rest of the economy,
notably through worsening payment delays and rising pressure on the
profitability of financial institutions.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.39 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Abroad, trading partners are becoming increasingly intolerant. This
is particularly the case of the United States and the European Union 4,
which have absorbed 24% and 28% respectively of China’s trade surplus
over the last twelve months (data from China’s General Administration
of Customs). Tensions between the US and China could rise again in
2027 when the current truce (which has mainly led to a reduction in

tariffs following the peak in H1 2025) comes to an end. Against a back-
drop of competition for technological supremacy and industrial sove -
reignty, the trend towards decoupling between China and the US could
continue, thereby reducing market opportunities for Chinese producers.
Europe, for its part, is reinforcing its strategic approach and expanding
its toolkit of measures, moving towards more targeted restrictions and
stricter market access conditions, especially in case of serious market

distortions). Rather than pursuing a strategy of decoupling, the EU is
seeking to reduce risks and provide greater protection for critical sec -
tors (including the automotive and chemical industries) without clo -
sing its market and whilst continuing to attract Chinese investment.
This strategy will also lead to tensions. With imbalances becoming
increasingly unsustainable, what course of action would make it pos -
sible to avoid a sudden adjustment precipitated by episodes of crises?

4 See BNP Paribas, EcoWeek, When it comes to China, Europe should focus less on its trade deficits and more on its investments
A ‘concerted’ adjustment is difficult to envisage. A negotiated adjust -
ment, along the lines of the 1985 Plaza agreements, seems unlikely in
the current international and military context. Furthermore, China and
its trading partners largely disagree on the diagnosis and the causes of
the imbalances. Moreover, a simple revaluation of the yuan would, on

its own, have only a limited effect on China’s competitiveness.
A gradual adjustment may be possible… in the long term. However, the
reduction of imbalances will need to come both from partner countries
(through increased savings) and from China. In the short term, China
can ease trade tensions by investing more abroad, which is all the
easier given that it has huge current account surpluses. This would
enable it to maintain its access to foreign markets and support the

production capacity of its trading partners. More structural changes
(a more flexible exchange rate regime, less fiscal support for indus -
tries and more support for households) will only be feasible once Bei -
jing considers that the costs of its export-led model will outweigh the
strategic gains. A key challenge for trading partners will be to work out
how to shift this cost-benefit balance.
Christine Peltier
christine.peltier@bnpparibas.com

聯發科 2454 TT ( 電子上游–IC 設計 ) 
件 ) 
金麟化龍，再登巔峰 
投資與評價 
1. ASIC 貢獻優於預期、以及 smart edge platform 挹注下，抵銷手機下
滑影響，預估 2026 年營收將年增 14.2%。 
2. 預估 ASIC 貢獻將於 2027 更加驚豔，上修營收貢獻至 5000 億台幣水
準，手機則呈現回溫態勢，預估 2027 年營收年增 83.3%。 
3. 預估聯發科 2026/2027 年 EPS 為 73.85/171.17 元，考量 ASIC 成長
性，AI 能見度高且延續性佳，以歷史本益比區間中間偏上位置 28 倍為
評價基礎，目標價 4800 元，維持買進投資評等。 
重點更新 
AI 成長優於預期，抵銷手機疲弱影響，上修 2026 年營收至年增 14.2% 
手機業務在 3Q26 年季減 1%，聯發科轉向高端機種來因應原物料成本上升
問題，在客戶陸續推出搭載聯發科 SoC 的新 AI 旗艦手機下，研究部修正預
估手機整年衰退約 16.2%。smart edge platforms 今年主要受惠於 AI 平板 、
AI PC 及車用座艙等專案量產，預估整年營收年增 13.5%，power IC 業務受
惠資料中心相關業務市占提升，研究部上修整年成長約為 5%。今年 AI ASIC
研究部預估貢獻從 20.8 億美元上修至 25.1 億美元營收，佔整體營收 11.4%
客戶需求優於預期，且獲得供應鏈的支援。整體而言，預估 2026 年營收隨
著在 AI ASIC 以及 smart edge platforms 貢獻下，可抵銷手機下滑影響，並
上修成長幅度，營收預估年增 14.2%，達 6804.5 億元，毛利率因成本上升以
及 ASIC 毛利較低，略為下滑至 46.2%。 
AI ASIC 迎來爆發期，算力需求令人驚艷，上修 2027 營收年增 83.3% 
AI ASIC 強進需求將延續至 2027 年整年，而根據調研下一代 ASIC 產品不管
在規模與定價皆高於第一個專案，推估將於 1Q28 進入量產 ，2027 年來自資
料中心 ASIC 營收上修至貢獻超過 5000 億台幣規模(約多 1200 億元)，營收
佔比將來到四成水準，佔比高於先前約 6%。而隨著 AI 導入生活以及相關應
用需求的增長下，算力及高效連結雲端為必備功能，手機部分 在低基期下預
估將成長約 23%。而在邊緣 AI 部分，隨著代理 AI 趨勢，邊緣設備迎來結構
性增長，預估維持高速成長態勢。研究部預估 2027 年營收隨著在 AI ASIC 爆
發及手機回溫下，營收將年增83.3%，突破一兆大關，並且毛利率雖因 ASIC
占比較高下略有稀釋，但 ASIC 營業利益率較高，整體提升至 23.17%。

資料來源：台灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 2

訪談內容 
需求毫無疑慮，成長順勢而起 
手機方面，AI 落地需求以及作為日常生活必需品，研究部認為代理 AI 創新加速發展，公司正與
Android 生態系合作，推動整合至 SoC，並看好其應用延伸至 wearable、mobile、IoT、PC 與
automotive。

在 smart edge platforms 方面，搭載 N1X 的 NVIDIA RTX Spark AI PC 於 10 月中旬透過主要
PC OEM 夥伴上市，結合 NVIDIA GPU 與聯發科 SoC，支援裝置端代理式 AI。Dimensity 9500
搭載於 Samsung Galaxy T ab S12系列 AI 平板，為 Samsung AI 平板連續第三代採用聯發科旗
艦 SoC。

AI ASIC 方面，第二款 AI 加速器封裝方案執行進展良好，目標較前代提高數倍每瓦效能並降低總
持有成本，部分晶粒已完成 tape-out，較大且複雜的晶粒仍依時程推進，將與美國 IDM 封裝夥
伴及台、日基板供應商合作改善良率，並爭取需求上行所需產能，研究部預估 1Q28 進入量產。

ASIC 亦已取得不同於既有主要客戶的第二家資料中心 ASIC 客戶，專案已啟動，預計2028 年開
始量產及貢獻營收， 另外資料中心業務將從晶片延伸至系統與機櫃 ， 透過NVIDIA NVLink Fusion
將客製化 XPU 接入 NVLink 生態系，並支援 UALink，降低部署複雜度、縮短上市與營收實現時
間，並持續推進高速互連、先進封裝與 CPO，448Gbps SerDes 已完成 tape-out，後續進行量
測、驗證及互通性開發，服務更廣泛客戶。

### Page 3

聯發科 ESG 政策 
聯發科主要從事 IC 設計 ， 產業類別歸類在半導體行業中 ， 依據專門為投資人設計的永續揭露框架 SASB
準則，半導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水管理、廢棄物和危險材料
管理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、材料採購與效率。由於聯發
科為無廠 IC 設計公司，研究部將篇幅著重在產品設計面，並因員工對於IC 設計公司而言是相當重要的
資產，故員工相關議題也會著重於下描述，其餘溫室氣體排放及水和廢水管理等則於後簡述。

1. 員工健康與安全： 
為確保承攬商施工安全管理 ， 對於入場施工之人員均辦理職業安全衛生講習 ， 進行危害告知及宣導安全
施工規範，以預防職業災害發生。2022 年參與安全講習之承攬商共有 793 人並達成年度零災害，零
意外、零損失之目標。

圖 1 員工失能、職業病、缺席比率

資料來源:聯發科、國泰證期研究部整理

2. 員工多元化和包容性： 
聯發科技重視研發創新及國際化營運，視全球人才布局為技術研發的驅動力。截至  2022 年年底，聯
發科技員工總人數為 18,993 人。為有效運籌全球研發能量，聯發科技在全球設有 32 個辦公據點，
海外人員數占總人數約 35.4%。期待透過全球人才交流合作，加速產品開發時程與技術支援即時性。

圖 2 員工年齡架構 
資料來源:聯發科、國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 4

3. 溫室氣體排放： 
聯發科技能資源使用所排放之溫室氣體，排放來源包含外購電力、公用設備、鍋爐、冷卻水塔、冰水主
機等，主要排放源為範疇二的外購電力占整體 93.27%。在公司營運規模持續成長的表現下，致力提高
能源使用效率與規劃再生能源使用，以降低公司營運所產生的溫室氣體排放量。2022 年度主要減量作
為包含持續提升資料中心之能效，及完成既有辦公室據點照明更新為 LED 等，以提高用電效率。

圖 3 2022 年溫室氣體排放情形

資料來源:聯發科、國泰證期研究部整理

4. 水和廢水管理： 
聯發科技之辦公室取水來自各區自來水廠供水，因今年度員工人數增加，且新建置 5 個辦公據點以及 
IT 資料中心之擴充 ， 共取用 485,629 公噸 （約 486 百萬公升） 水量 ， 其取水量相較於前一年增加 27%，
也因此無法達成 2021 年所設定之目標 5%，雖總用水量提升，聯發科技持續進行水資源項目改善計
畫，平均節水比例達 3.7%，未來將持續搭配進行各項用水減量措施，達成每年減少用水 5% 的目標。
廢水主要為生活污水 ， 依各區納管統一由廢水處理廠處理後排放至自然水體 ， 不會對當地環境產生影響
與衝擊，2022 年排放 202,792 公噸（約 203 百萬公升）廢水，其廢水量相較於前一年增加12%，與
前述用水量增加之原因相同。

圖 4 用水量、節水量與節水比例圖

《工業REITs營運改善，但利率走升壓抑估值》
3Q26工業REITs財報受惠電子商務 、先進製造及數
據中心供應鏈建設支撐需求 ，客戶庫存保持低水平 。
現金租金 /淨有效租金差價變動率為 23.4%/38%，
定價力仍強 ，季末入住率為 95.6%，較上季增加 ，
今年入住率雖收窄上緣0.25%至95.25%-95.5%，但
現 金 同 店 淨營 業收 入 從6.75%–7.25% 上調至 7%-
7.5%，預估SS NOI淨有效基礎上調0.25%至5.5%-
6%。策略資本收入略降至 6.5-6.6億美元 ，資本部
署中上調開發案穩定 、新開工開發案 、收購，但利
率走升將壓抑物業交易活動及估值擴張 ，將放緩資
產處分以應對 ，但維持開發利潤金額 。產業新增供
給量放緩，預估今年FFO年增率約7-8%。

利差優勢消長，新興與商品

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
高油價、美元偏強及全球景氣分化持續牽動新興與
商品貨幣。澳洲就業轉弱、通膨改善，澳洲央行升
息週期料暫告一段落，澳幣短線料區間盤整。紐西
蘭央行意外按兵不動削弱紐幣短期動能，但升息預
期仍提供支撐。加拿大央行政策轉鷹及高油價利好
加幣，惟經濟與貿易不確定性限制升值空間。中國
內需偏弱，但出口暢旺、企業結匯需求強，加上政
策對升值容忍度提高，人民幣料溫和升值。南非幣
受美元、高油價及利差收窄壓抑，但財政紀律改善、
信用前景轉佳及實質利率優勢有助緩和貶值壓力，
料維持區間震盪。

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投

《投資關鍵 – 澳洲景氣放緩、澳央轉向觀望，預期
澳幣盤整》
隨著國際油價維持高位，澳洲央行（RBA）於9月再
度升息將現金利率提高至 4.6%，官員雖表示若通膨
壓力持續，不排除進一步緊縮。
但隨著高利率與高通膨壓力持續拖累經濟動能，近期
公布的 9月就業意外減少 3.93萬人 、失業率更升至
4.7%，已連續兩個月落在澳央主席認為可有效壓抑
通膨的失業率4.5~5.0%區間；通膨方面，9月份澳洲
CPI雖由4.0%升至4.3%，但核心CPI僅有2.2%，加以
住房通膨明顯回落，因此本行預期澳央轉向觀望、本
輪升息暫時告終 ，2027年政策轉向觀望，如國際油
價回落至90美元以下，澳央有降息空間。
外需方面，中國經濟基本面持續低迷，消費與投資不

振亦難以挹注澳洲出口動能，外需對澳幣支撐力量有
限，加以預期聯準會在 12月再度升息 ，將呈現「利
差有撐但優勢遞減」格局，因此預估澳幣短線維持在
0.7~0.71區間盤整，要進一步突破或要待2Q27美國
通膨回落、降息預期再度升溫，方有走揚力量。

觀望 ，澳幣維持在 0.7~0.71

紐西蘭通膨升溫訊號浮現，月度物價年增 5.2%
《投資關鍵 - 央行意外按兵不動拖累紐元 ，後續升息
預期提供下檔支撐》
過去一個月 ，受美元強勢 、美債殖利率及油價走高拖
累，紐幣兌美元一度下探 0.5565，其後隨升息預期升
溫而回穩 。惟紐西蘭央行 10月28日意外維持 OCR於
2.75%不變，促使市場下修短期升息預期 ，紐幣再度
承壓。
紐西蘭經濟延續溫和復甦 ，企業信心及農產品價格改
善，惟勞動市場仍有閒置產能 。雖然整體通膨低於預
期，但核心通膨及價格廣度上升 ，加上油價高企與紐

幣偏弱，我們預期4Q26通膨仍具黏性，價格壓力擴散
風險猶存。
我們仍維持偏鷹派的政策基準情境 。10月以4比3票按
兵不動，較反映升息時點後移 ，而非整體政策路徑轉
向，緊縮週期尚未結束。我們仍預期12月及明年3月具
升息空間 ，尤其若通膨 、油價及匯率壓力持續 ，政策
立場料維持偏緊。
展望4Q26，10月意外按兵不動削弱紐幣利差支撐，我
們仍看好紐元中期修復空間 ，惟短期上行動能有所減
弱。升息預期降溫 ，加上大選 、美元及全球風險情緒
等不確定性，料使匯價維持較高波動。進入1Q27後，
隨政策不確定性逐步下降 ，經濟溫和復甦有望成為紐
幣主要支撐 。考量匯價已反映相當程度的成長疑慮及
外部利空 ，若基本面持續改善 ，紐幣中期仍具修復空
間。

### Page 4

通膨數據回落，6月降息的可能性加大重點摘要 《投資關鍵 - 央行轉鷹支撐加幣，惟經濟及貿易風險
限制升值空間》
能源價格高企令加拿大通膨面臨上行風險，加拿大央
行政策預期轉鷹亦推動加美利差劣勢收窄，支持加幣
自10月中旬低點反彈 。不過，隨本次25個基點升息
已大致反映於市場定價，加幣能否延續升勢將更取決
於央行對後續緊縮路徑的指引，以及市場對終端利率
的重新定價，而非本次升息本身。
加拿大央行最新升息 25個基點至2.5%，為逾三年來
首次升息，並表示若能源價格進一步推升廣泛通膨壓
力，不排除繼續收緊政策 。央行亦將 2027年通膨預
測由 2.0%上調至 3.1%，並將今年經濟成長預測由
0.7%上調至1.0%，短期政策訊號偏鷹 。惟核心通膨
仍相對溫和，勞動市場、製造業需求及招聘意願亦呈
轉弱跡象，意味若經濟活動進一步降溫，持續升息空
間或有限。高油價及偏高的投機性加幣空頭部位仍構
成短期利好，但美加貿易不確定性可能繼續拖累企業
投資及成長前景。因此，我們維持4Q26至1Q27加幣

中性看法。短期加美利差收窄及油價高企有望限制加
幣下行風險，但在增長前景改善或央行釋出更明確的
持續緊縮訊號前，加幣進一步升值空間有限。

### Page 5

，6月降息的可能性加大重點摘要
• 從景氣趨勢、政策取向與國
際經貿局勢變化三大視角切
入，綜合判斷人民幣溫和升
值的看法不變。
《投資關鍵 – 政經拉鋸與景氣K型下，人民幣溫和升值》
中國公布最新 3Q26 GDP成長4.5%，出口年增率升至
28.4%，貿易順差由前季的3148.6億美元擴大至3651.2
億美元 。市場預估中國 2026年全年貿易順差將超越
2025年1.2兆美元紀錄，再創新高。惟中國外需繁榮卻
無法掩蓋內需疲軟困境 ，中國3Q26零售銷售年增率僅
0.7%，固定資產投資衰退7%。儘管中國陸續推出穩增
長政策，大多以財政政策為主軸，貨幣政策偏向定向式
而非全面降準降息。因此儘管美啟動升息，中美利差尚
保持穩定，因此內需疲軟與有限度的寬鬆貨幣政策對人
民幣走勢並未形成顯著壓力。
中國的出口暢旺除受惠科技週期貢獻外，強勁汽車出口
至歐洲也是主因，9月中國汽車出口維持62%的高成長。
惟加劇保護主義傾向，中歐達成初步協議，未來四年內
將對歐洲的混合動力車出口量減少一半以上，具體配額、

最低售價及豁免條件仍待協商。政策角度觀察，人民幣
升值本身可能成為中國應對外部壓力的重要工具，減緩
低估匯率促進出口的國際批評。
考量關稅可能成為攻防戰手段之一，研判中國廠商或搶
出口支持外貿表現高檔，同時官方可能對人民幣升值空
間容忍態度提高 ，疊加前三季結售匯順差高達 3,941億
美元，創2010年以來新高，顯示企業結匯意願強勁，持
續為人民幣提供支撐。從景氣趨勢、政策取向與國際經
貿局勢變化的三大視角切入，綜合判斷人民幣溫和升值
的看法不變。

### Page 6

《投資關鍵 - 基本面改善但部位趨擁擠，南非幣料維
持震盪》
南非幣第四季以來震盪偏弱，USD/ZAR近期回升至約
16.60。外部環境仍偏不利，高油價推升進口成本與
通膨壓力，加上美元偏強及美國南非利差收窄，削弱
南非幣套息吸引力。與此同時，巴西總統大選結果引
發市場波動，增添新興市場不確定性，短期投資人風
險偏好轉趨審慎，亦可能加重新興市場貨幣的資金流
出壓力。
基本面方面，南非財政紀律改善、主權信用前景轉佳，
加上較高實質利率及具吸引力的利差，仍為南非幣提
供支撐。惟高油價及聖嬰現象相關極端天氣風險令通
膨壓力偏高，限制政策寬鬆空間。另一方面，近期投
機性及資產管理機構多頭部位明顯增加，顯示市場部
位漸趨擁擠。在外部風險升溫及風險偏好轉弱下，獲
利了結與部位調整可能放大短期波動，即使匯價回檔，
逢低布局的風險報酬吸引力仍相對有限。
整體而言，南非幣基本面有所改善，但短期外部環境
與部位面均偏不利。財政紀律改善、主權信用前景轉
佳及實質利率優勢有助建立匯率下檔支撐，惟強勢美
元、高油價、利差收窄及偏擁擠的多頭部位限制升值

空間。預期4Q26美元兌南非幣仍以區間震盪為主，維
持南非幣中性看法。
重點摘要
資料來源：CFTC，Bloomberg，2026/9，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10

3Q26 獲利不如預期，4Q26 產能利用率 90~95%、報價持平 
3Q26 整體產能利用率提升至 92%，但 ASP 持平，營收略低於預期 2.8%，
且因生產成本上升、毛利率 34%低於財測的 35%，稅後 EPS 為 1.32 元，
低於原預估的 1.72 元。4Q26 AI 需求續強，手機需求仍偏弱、但聯電市占提
升，車用、通訊復甦，電腦運算相關需求則維持疲弱，整體產能利用率維持
90~95%的滿載水準，晶圓出貨預估季增 0~5%，ASP 則持平，因價格未有
明顯變化，毛利率將與 3Q26 持平。將 4Q26 營收預估下修 0.3%至 783.8
億元，季增 5.6%、年增 26.8%，毛利率由原預估的 36.4%下調至 34.5%，
加上業外收益減少，稅後 EPS 預估為 1.47 元、下修 17.4%。 
追加 32 億美元資金擴產，2027 年 ASP 將增加 10% 
追加 32 億美元資金以增加新加坡 12i 及台南 12A 產能，新應用中的矽光子
預計產能 1.8 萬片(新加坡)、DTC 矽電容產能 2.2 萬片(台南)、TFLN 產能 2
萬片 (8 吋廠房)，皆預計 2H28 開始貢獻營收，並預期 2030 年達到規劃規模。
公司原預估 2026/2027 年折舊皆將年增 10~15%，目前對 2027 年折舊看法
為年增約 5%。來自 AI PMIC 、FPGA、網路連接、矽光子等需求仍強勁，
有利產品組合優化、亦有助改善訂價環境，且因應原料成本增加，聯電預期
2027 年平均 ASP 將年增約 10%，且 1Q27 即開始反映部分價格調整。 
2027 年本業獲利將年增超過 60%，維持買進 
預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 為 7.43 元/7.93 元，分別下修 9%/上修 2%，扣
除 1H26 業外投資收益約 2.58 元後，2026 年稅後 EPS 為 4.85 元，2027 年
產能利用率及 ASP 提升，預估營收將年增 19.7%、本業獲利則將年增 60%
以上，營運重回成長軌道，考量 2028 年將切入 FinFET 及光通訊相關應用，
故維持買進評等，目標價調升至 160 元(20X 2027F EPS)。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
2H26 產能利用率維持 90~95%滿載水準，但 ASP 皆持平，3Q26 獲利
不如預期。4Q26 獲利則下修 17.4%至 1.47 元。 
⧫  
追加 32 億美元資金增加新加坡 12i 廠矽光子、台南 12A 廠矽電容、8
吋廠 TFLN 產能，預計 2H28 開始貢獻營收。 
⧫  
2027 年營收將年增 19.7%、本業獲利有望年增超過 60%，營運重回成
長軌道，維持買進建議、目標價調升至 160 元(20X 2027F EPS)。

營運分析  
3Q26 產能利用率提升，但獲利不如預期 
3Q26 整體產能利用率提升至 92%，晶圓出貨 121.2 萬片，季增 7.4%、年增 21.2%，ASP 持平，
營收略低於預期 2.8%，其中 DDIC、車用雷達、WiFi 等需求帶動 22/28 奈米營收比重 35%、產能
維持滿載，但因生產成本上升、毛利率 34%低於財測的 35%，亦低於原預估的 36.3%，加上業外
收益減少，稅後 EPS 為 1.32 元，低於原預估的 1.72 元。

### Page 3

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：晶圓代工 2026 年 10 月 29 日 UMC 第3 頁，共 14頁
4Q26 產能利用率維持 90~95%，但 ASP 持平、獲利下修 17.4%
4Q26 AI 需求續強，手機需求仍偏弱、但聯電市占提升，車用、通訊復甦，電腦運算相關需求則維
持疲弱，整體產能利用率維持 90~95%的滿載水準，而新加坡廠產能擴充、整體產能季增約 0.9%
至 133.7 萬片，將帶動晶圓出貨季增 0~5%，ASP 則持平，因價格未有明顯變化，毛利率將與
3Q26 持平。將 4Q26 營收預估下修 0.3%至 783.8 億元，季增 5.6%、年增 26.8%，毛利率由原預
估的 36.4%下調至 34.5%，加上業外收益減少，稅後 EPS 預估為 1.47 元、下修 17.4%。

追加 32 億美元資金擴產，2027 年 ASP 將增加 10%
AI 相關的 PMIC、連接通訊等應用具成長動能，2026 年營收貢獻近 3 億美元、約占營收 3%，聯電
看好至 2030 年有望達到 10 億美元營收，且 2029 年有望占到營收的 25%。2026 年資本支出維持
20 億美元，為因應後續需求，董事會通過追加 32 億美元資金以增加新加坡 12i 及台南 12A 產能，
新應用中的矽光子預計產能 1.8 萬片(新加坡)、DTC 矽電容產能 2.2 萬片(台南)、TFLN 產能 2 萬片
(8 吋廠房)，皆預計 2H28 開始貢獻營收，並預期 2030 年達到規劃規模。
公司原預估 2026/2027 年折舊皆將年增 10~15%，目前對 2027 年折舊看法為年增約 5%。來自 AI
PMIC、FPGA、網路連接、矽光子等需求仍強勁，有利產品組合優化、亦有助改善訂價環境，且因

應原料成本增加，聯電預期 2027 年平均 ASP 將年增約 10%，且 1Q27 即開始反映部分價格調整。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：晶圓代工 2026 年 10 月 29 日 UMC 第4 頁，共 14頁  
產業概況  
台灣晶圓代工產值年增 34%，成熟製程產業狀況改善 
台灣半導體產業持續受惠 AI 需求發展，先進製程優先受惠，預估 2026 年台灣晶圓代工產業產值年
增 35~40%，而台積電的市占率約 94%，營收成長仍優於其他晶圓代工同業，而台積電規劃將 6 吋、
8 吋成熟製程產能轉作利潤更高的先進封裝，預估 2027 年每月將有約 9 萬片的產能釋出，約可提
升聯電、世界先進的成熟製程產能利用率 10~15%，其中世界的新加坡 12 吋廠預計 2027 年投產，
較有望承接來自台積電的轉單效益，預估可增加 1~1.5 元 EPS，受惠程度較大。而整體成熟製程
仍受惠 AI 需求爆發所帶來的外溢效果，產業狀況持續改善。

獲利調整與股票評價  
2027 年本業獲利將年增超過 60%，維持買進 
成熟製程市場需求狀況改善，儘管消費性電子需求仍疲弱，但 AI、車用、工控等需求仍帶動聯電產
能利用率提升及產品組合改善，將有利於聯電的獲利表現，加上新加坡廠及台南廠開始進行擴產計
畫，整體營運已開始復甦。 
預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 為 7.43 元/7.93 元，分別下修 9%/上修 2%，扣除 1H26 業外投資收益
約 2.58 元後，2026 年稅後 EPS 為 4.85 元，2027 年產能利用率及 ASP 提升，預估營收將年增
19.7%、本業獲利則將年增 60%以上，營運重回成長軌道，考量 2028 年將切入 FinFET 及光通訊
相關應用，故維持買進評等，目標價調升至 160 元(20X 2027F EPS，由前次 16X 調升)。

### Page 9

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：晶圓代工 2026 年 10 月 29 日 UMC 第9 頁，共 14頁

公司簡介  
聯電設立於 1980 年，主要業務為晶圓製造服務，其製程以 22nm 以上的成熟製程為主。主要廠區分布於台灣、中國廈門、
日本以及新加坡。客戶包含聯發科、 Qualcomm、Samsung、NXP、德州儀器等國際大廠。公司發展策略不同於台積電，
其以晶圓製造服務為基礎，轉投資多家半導體晶片設計公司，並以自有產能及技術扶植半導體晶片設計公司，而當半導體
晶片設計公司之產品在市場中具競爭優勢取得需求量時，亦將回饋聯電，得以維持晶圓代工產能利用率，案例如聯陽、聯
詠、智原、原相、盛群等 IC 設計及設計服務公司。

### Page 11

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：晶圓代工 2026 年 10 月 29 日 UMC 第11 頁，共 14頁

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 聯電整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於領先的位置，
在晶圓代工的公司中排名略微領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 聯電的整體曝險屬於中等水準，相當於晶圓代工行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的  ESG 議
題包含水資源運用、自身營運、商業道德與原物料使用等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：聯電在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力強。公司已指定董事會成員負責監督
ESG（環境、社會和治理）事務。此外，公司在環境議題上的政策非常完善。公司每年發布一份符合最佳實踐標準的可
持續發展報告。聯電還建立了一套完善的舉報人保護機制。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

7. 10/28 美國 9 ⽉商品貿易逆差擴⼤⾄ 1,349 億美元，資本財進⼝增 7.5%
8. 10/28 川普簽署公告，認定進⼝機器⼈與⼯業機械危及國安
9. 10/28 中國⼈⾏：第三季末房地產貸款餘額年減 5.2%
10. 10/28 渣打第三季稅前盈餘 23 億美元年增 29% ，上調全年營收指引
事件  1
10/28 聯準會維持利率 3.75% ⾄ 4.00% ，庫克與哈瑪克主張升息
五⼤重點
1   聯準會 10/28 決議維持聯邦資⾦利率⽬標區間 3.75% ⾄ 4.00% ，為 9 ⽉升息 1 碼後⾸度暫停；表決
10 ⽐ 2 ，理事庫克與克⾥夫蘭聯準銀⾏總裁哈瑪克主張再升 1 碼，庫克為 2022 年就任後⾸度投反
對票。
2   聲明將經濟擴張步調由「穩健」上調為「強勁」，刪除 9 ⽉「本次⾏動有助通膨更及時回到 2% 」

⼀句，改為「累積更多資訊將有助判斷適當政策⽴場」，並重申通膨仍偏⾼。

### Page 2

3   主席華許在記者會說明， 9 ⽉升息後部分企業與家庭的⾦融與信⽤條件已收緊，但他不願把整
體⾦融條件稱為緊縮；他重申平時不提供前瞻指引，不預判 12 ⽉決策。
4   決議後 10 年期公債殖利率維持在 5.028% 附近， 2 年期在記者會後回落，美元指數收跌 0.11% ⾄
102.211 ；利率期貨隱含 12 ⽉升息 1 碼機率由會前約 82% 降⾄約 78.4% 。
5   華許宣布⾃ 2027 年秋季起調整會議制度：每年仍開 8 次會， 2027 年其中 1 次專談貨幣政策策
略、不表決利率也不發聲明， 2028 年增⾄ 2 次，利率決策會議將減為 6 次。
三⼤結論
1   聲明刪除升息成效的措辭、改由資料決定，顯⽰ 9 ⽉升息後聯準會進⼊觀察期；但兩票主張升
息與「通膨仍偏⾼」的判斷，代表暫停不等於轉向寬鬆，市場不宜把按兵不動外推為降息。
2   華許把 AI 類股震盪與信⽤利差擴⼤視為升息傳導的⼀部分，代表⾦融條件收緊可替代部分升
息； 10 年期殖利率仍在 5% 上⽅，⻑天期公債的期限⾵險未解除，短天期國庫券仍適合承接政策

不確定性。
3   當 12 ⽉會議前核⼼ PCE （個⼈消費⽀出物價指數）、服務價格與油價同步⾛⾼，或 10 年期殖利
率再創新⾼時，應縮短存續期間並降低⾼估值成⻑股；若信⽤條件續緊⽽就業轉弱，才逐步把短
券到期資⾦轉向⻑債。
事件  2
10/28 加拿⼤央⾏升息 25 個基點⾄ 2.50% ，三年多來⾸度升息
五⼤重點
1   加拿⼤央⾏ 10/28 將隔夜利率⽬標調升 25 個基點⾄ 2.50% ，銀⾏利率 2.75% 、存款利率 2.45% ，
為三年多來⾸度升息，符合市場預期；下次決議為 12/9 。
2   央⾏預估 CPI （消費者物價指數）今年底升⾄約 3.5% ， 2027 年平均約 3% ，較 7 ⽉預測⾼ 1 個百
分點， 2028 年中才回到 2% ； 9 ⽉剔除汽油的通膨為 2.2% ，核⼼通膨指標仍接近 2% 。
3   央⾏說明升息是為降低⾼能源價格擴散⾄其他商品與服務的⾵險；其企業調查顯⽰未來⼀年成
本轉嫁將增加，近期通膨預期上升，⽽中東衝突已進⼊第⼋個⽉、尚無明確解⽅。

4   第⼆季 GDP 季增年率 3.3% ，第三季約 2% ，均強於 7 ⽉預估，失業率連續數⽉約 6.5% ；央⾏預
期美國新關稅將拖累第四季成⻑， 2027 年再由企業投資、出⼝與財政措施帶動回升。

### Page 3

5   聲明指出「可能需要進⼀步升息」，但委員會將審慎⾏事；決議後美元兌加幣由 1.4052 降⾄
1.4021 ，加幣⼩幅⾛強。
三⼤結論
1   加拿⼤在核⼼通膨接近 2% 下仍升息，理由是能源價格擴散⾵險，與聯準會、 ECB （歐洲央
⾏）、⽇銀及印度央⾏今年先後升息⽅向⼀致，顯⽰能源型通膨正把主要央⾏推向限制性⽴場，
全球降息預期短期難以重啟。
2   央⾏直接把⾼油價寫進升息理由，代表原油價格已左右利率路徑；原油宜定位為事件避險⽽⾮
⻑期基準，若航運恢復、庫存回升使油價回落，各國升息壓⼒與能源部位都應同步下修。
3   當 12/9 前加拿⼤與美國 CPI 中能源以外項⽬持續加速時，應預期北美利率維持⾼檔並保留短天
期國庫券；若中東航運恢復、油價跌回每桶 100 美元下⽅且通膨預期回落，再評估延⻑存續期
間。
事件  3
10/28 Alphabet 第三季每股盈餘 2.12 美元遜於預期，盤後重挫逾 10%
五⼤重點
1  Alphabet 10/28 公布第三季營收 1,276 億美元，年增 25% ，營業利益 406 億美元，年增 30% ，營

益率 32% ；但稀釋每股盈餘 2.12 美元，低於去年同期 2.87 美元與市場預估 3.01 美元。
2   獲利落差主因業外淨損失 82 億美元，其中權益證券淨損失 87 億美元，使每股盈餘減少 0.55 美
元；公司說明損失來⾃持有的 SpaceX 股價下跌，利息費⽤則由 1.43 億美元升⾄ 9.67 億美元。
3  Google 雲端營收 307 億美元，年增 103% ，營業利益 111 億美元，⾼管說明 TPU 系統銷售是雲端
成⻑主要來源；但搜尋營收 634 億美元、年增 12% ， YouTube 廣告 112 億美元、年增 9% （推算）。
4   第三季資本⽀出 514 億美元，單季⾃由現⾦流為負 91 億美元，近 12 個⽉⾃由現⾦流 197 億美
元、年減 73% ；財務⻑說明營運現⾦流受 TPU 備貨庫存增加拖累，股價盤後重挫逾 10% 。
5   公司維持 2026 年資本⽀出 1,950 億⾄ 2,050 億美元，並預期 2027 年⼤幅增加；未來採購承諾達

9,240 億美元，另有 1,520 億美元租賃及其他義務。
三⼤結論
1   本業營益率仍升⾄ 32% ，每股盈餘落差主要來⾃持股評價損失，但市場以盤後重挫逾 10% 回
應，顯⽰投資⼈已從看營收轉為檢驗 AI 投資的⾃由現⾦流，雲端業者必須通過資本報酬與現⾦流
驗證。

### Page 4

2   雲端營收倍增且 TPU 系統銷售成為成⻑來源，⽀持晶圓代⼯與先進封裝等上游需求；但單季⾃
由現⾦流轉負、採購承諾逾 9 千億美元，代表下游資本⽀出⾵險正在累積，不宜把需求直接等同
於獲利。
3   當雲端業者資本⽀出續增⽽⾃由現⾦流連兩季為負、庫存增加快於營收時，應降低雲端與⾼估
值 Nasdaq 部位；若雲端營益率續升且資本⽀出回收改善，可維持先進製程與先進封裝核⼼配
置。
事件  4
10/28 微軟 Azure 營收年增 48% 優於預期，盤後⼀度⼤漲逾 6%
五⼤重點
1   微軟 10/28 公布 2027 財年第⼀季（ 7 ⾄ 9 ⽉）營收 923 億美元，年增 19% ；營業利益 459 億美元，
年增 21% ，⾮ GAAP 每股盈餘 4.89 美元，⾼於市場預估 4.73 美元。
2  Azure 營收 332 億美元，年增 48% ，⾼於市場預估 44.8% ，並較上季 43% 加速；微軟雲營收 639
億美元，年增 30% ，按固定匯率計年增 31% 。
3   現⾦資本⽀出 394 億美元，低於市場預估 459 億美元；財務⻑說明部分第三⽅產能交付延後，

約四分之三資本⽀出⽤於 CPU 與 GPU 等短週期資產，股價盤後⼀度⼤漲逾 6% 。
4   財測第⼆財季營收 960 億⾄ 971 億美元、 Azure 固定匯率成⻑ 49% ⾄ 50% ，資本⽀出逾 500 億美
元；財務⻑預估 2026 曆年資本⽀出約 1,700 億美元。
5   商業剩餘履約義務（ RPO ） 6,770 億美元，年增 73% ，但低於市場預估 7,056 億美元且與上季持
平；⾃由現⾦流約 186 億美元，較去年同期下降約 27% （推算）。
三⼤結論
1  Azure 加速⾄ 48% 且資本⽀出低於預期，顯⽰微軟的 AI 投⼊已轉成收⼊，是本季雲端業者中資
本報酬較清楚的案例，⽀持軟體與雲端以 RPO 、客戶留存與⾃由現⾦流選股，⽽⾮只看資本⽀
出規模。
2   資本⽀出低於預期部分源⾃第三⽅產能交付延後⽽⾮需求放緩，第⼆財季⽀出將逾 500 億美
元，對伺服器、記憶體與先進封裝供應鏈是訂單延續訊號；但營收認列時點可能遞延，需以出貨
與回款確認。
3   當 RPO 連續兩季持平、⾃由現⾦流持續年減時，應放慢雲端類股加碼速度；若 Azure 成⻑維持

近 50% 且⾃由現⾦流回升，可在雲端與軟體中提⾼現⾦流穩健的⼤型業者⽐重。

### Page 5

事件  5
10/28 英國預算案：本財年⾚字上修⾄ GDP 的 4.2% ，銀⾏附加稅升⾄ 6%
五⼤重點
1   英國財政⼤⾂希利 10/28 提出⾸份預算案，預算責任辦公室（ OBR ）將 2026/27 財年公共部⾨淨
借款上修⾄ GDP 的 4.2% （ 3 ⽉預測 3.6% ）， 2027/28 財年上修⾄ 3.4% （原 2.9% ）。
2  OBR 估計中東衝突使今年公債利息⽀出增加 140 億英鎊，並將 2027 年 GDP 成⻑預測由 1.6% 下
修⾄ 1.0% ；財政規則緩衝在 2029/30 財年為 150 億英鎊， 2030/31 財年升⾄ 220 億英鎊。
3   銀⾏附加稅⾃明年 1 ⽉起由 3% 調升⾄ 6% ，適⽤利潤逾 1 億英鎊部分、為期 15 個⽉，⽤於能源帳
單補貼；市場先前擔⼼恢復⾄ 8% ，宣布後銀⾏股反彈。
4   能源措施包括向 2,000 萬戶家庭發放每戶 100 英鎊補貼、總額 26 億英鎊，並延⻑加值稅減免⾄明
年 7 ⽉；希利表⽰天然氣價格⾃ 2 ⽉以來漲近 3 倍。

5   債務管理辦公室計畫 2026/27 財年發⾏ 2,506 億英鎊公債，略⾼於市場預期 2,492 億英鎊； 10 年
期公債殖利率在演說期間下跌 1.4 個基點⾄ 5.34% 。
三⼤結論
1   ⾚字上修主因能源衝擊與利息⽀出增加，⽽⾮⼤規模減稅，英國仍維持財政整頓路徑，使公債
發⾏量接近預期、殖利率未失控；但 5.3% 以上的 10 年期殖利率代表英國已接近利率⾼於名⽬成
⻑的壓⼒區。
2   銀⾏附加稅加幅低於市場擔憂且屬暫時措施，讓銀⾏股獲得緩解，但稅負增加仍壓縮英國銀⾏
獲利；配置上英國⻑天期公債不宜作核⼼防守，歐股以低負債、⾼現⾦流公司為主。
3   當能源補貼擴⼤或成⻑下修使⾚字再偏離 OBR 路徑、公債標售轉弱時，應降低英國⻑天期公
債與英鎊集中曝險；若⾚字依 3.4% 路徑下降且通膨回落，再評估分段增加存續期間。
事件  6
10/28 歐洲天然氣期貨突破每百萬⽡時 100 歐元， 2022 年來⾸⾒
五⼤重點
1   歐洲基準荷蘭 TTF 天然氣期貨 10/28 盤中最多上漲 3.7% ，台灣時間 10/28 21:36 ⾸度觸及每百萬

⽡時 100 歐元，為 2022 年能源危機以來⾸⾒，並於 10/29 凌晨突破 100 歐元。

### Page 6

2   漲勢主因市場擔⼼伊朗衝突升級進⼀步收緊全球天然氣供給，加上歐洲庫存偏低、北半球進⼊
供暖旺季；美國被指已通知波斯灣國家為期中選舉後的短促軍事⾏動做準備，尚未獲官⽅證實。
3   原油同步⾛⾼， WTI 12 ⽉期貨 10/28 上漲 2.88% ，收每桶 102.78 美元，重回 100 美元之上
（ 10/27 曾收 99.90 美元），布蘭特收 115.32 美元、漲 1.26% 。
4   義⼤利 9 ⽉⽣產者物價年增 13.8% ，為 2022 年 12 ⽉以來最⾼，剔除能源僅年增 3.5% ，顯⽰歐洲
⽣產成本上升集中在能源，尚未全⾯擴散。
5   美國監管機構 10/28 提出新規，加快液化天然氣（ LNG ）專案審批並擴⼤出⼝產能，是歐洲中
⻑期增加供給的主要來源，但短期無法填補今年冬季缺⼝。
三⼤結論
1   天然氣衝破 100 歐元、原油重回百元，代表能源衝擊在冬季前再度升溫，歐洲通膨與 ECB 政策
的約束加重； ECB 將於 10/29 公布決議（預期），能源價格是其難以轉向寬鬆的主因。

2   天然氣價格反映庫存、氣候與地緣的季節性緊張，宜以季節交易看待，不追逐⻑期缺⼝；歐股
以低負債、⾼現⾦流與國防基建受益者為主，避開能源密集的週期製造業。
3   當 TTF 持穩 100 歐元以上、 LNG 供給未同步增加且歐洲庫存持續偏低時，可⼩幅提⾼天然氣與
能源事件避險部位；若中東航運恢復或暖冬使價格回落，應逐步獲利了結。
事件  7
10/28 美國 9 ⽉商品貿易逆差擴⼤⾄ 1,349 億美元，資本財進⼝增 7.5%
五⼤重點
1   美國商務部 10/28 公布 9 ⽉先期商品貿易逆差 1,349 億美元，較 8 ⽉修正後的 1,321 億美元擴⼤
2.1% ，⾼於市場預期 1,302 億美元，為 2025 年 3 ⽉以來最⼤。
2  9 ⽉商品進⼝⽉增 2.4% 、出⼝⽉增 2.6% ；其中資本財進⼝躍升 7.5% ，這個類別涵蓋⽀撐 AI 基
礎設施的電腦及零組件、半導體與電信設備。
3   同⽇公布 9 ⽉批發庫存⽉增 0.6% ⾄ 9,415 億美元，⾼於預期 0.5% ，為連續第 8 個⽉增加；零售庫

存⽉增 0.3% ，與前⽉持平。
4   淨出⼝在 GDP 中為扣項， 9 ⽉逆差擴⼤將削弱第三季淨出⼝貢獻；但資本財進⼝增加，也代表
企業設備投資同步⾛強，兩者對 GDP 的影響⽅向相反。

### Page 7

5   本次為先期數據，完整的商品與服務貿易報告公布時可能修正；美國第三季 GDP 初值尚未公
布，淨出⼝與庫存變動將是影響成⻑數字的主要項⽬。
三⼤結論
1   資本財進⼝⽉增 7.5% ，顯⽰美國資料中⼼建置仍⼤量⾃海外採購電腦、伺服器與半導體，與
台灣 9 ⽉出⼝年增 60.9% 、對美出⼝年增 106.2% 相互印證，⽀持台灣 AI 伺服器與先進製程出⼝維
持⾼檔。
2   批發庫存連 8 個⽉增加，若主要反映 AI 設備備貨，屬主動補庫存延續；但庫存累積快於銷售
時，伺服器組裝與通路的營運資⾦壓⼒會先升⾼， ODM 需以應收帳款與存貨週轉驗證。
3   當美國資本財進⼝與台灣外銷訂單連續兩期轉弱、⽽批發庫存仍上升時，應先降低 ODM 與⼀
般 PCB 部位；若進⼝與訂單維持⾼增且庫存週轉改善，可維持先進製程與先進封裝核⼼。
事件  8
10/28 川普簽署公告，認定進⼝機器⼈與⼯業機械危及國安
五⼤重點
1   川普 10/28 簽署公告，依《貿易擴張法》第 232 條調整機器⼈、⼯業機械及其零組件進⼝，認定

相關進⼝構成國家安全⾵險，但未⽴即課徵關稅。
2   公告指⽰美國貿易代表與商務部⻑和各貿易夥伴談判協議，以處理來⾃任何國家的相關進⼝威
脅；這項第 232 條調查於 2025 年 9 ⽉啟動。
3   ⽩宮表⽰未來可能考慮其他補救措施，包括課徵或調整關稅、稅負、配額與費⽤，以及制定計
畫⿎勵在美國新建機器⼈與⼯業機械產能。
4   公告未設定談判期限，也未公布關稅稅率與適⽤產品清單，出⼝商短期⾯對的是政策不確定
性，⽽⾮⽴即增加的成本。
5   台灣 9 ⽉機械出⼝年增 30.7% 、半導體設備出⼝年增 61.0% ，若後續關稅涵蓋⼯業機械零組件，
台灣機械與設備出⼝將直接受影響。
三⼤結論
1   公告先以國安名義保留關稅選項、再以談判爭取協議，使機器⼈與⼯業機械成為下⼀個可能被
課稅的產業；在稅率公布前，出⼝國⾯對的主要是訂單排程與投資地點的不確定性。

### Page 8

2   台灣機械與半導體設備出⼝正受 AI 擴產帶動，若關稅落地，成本轉嫁能⼒與在美產能將成為分
化關鍵；具技術驗證與定價權的設備供應商較能吸收衝擊，標準品業者壓⼒較⼤。
3   當美國公布稅率或產品清單且涵蓋⼯業機械零組件時，應降低對美出⼝⽐重⾼、缺乏在美產能
的機械與設備衛星部位；若協議提供豁免或配額，則維持以訂單與⽑利篩選的現有配置。
事件  9
10/28 中國⼈⾏：第三季末房地產貸款餘額年減 5.2%
五⼤重點
1   中國⼈⺠銀⾏ 10/28 公布，第三季末⼈⺠幣各項貸款餘額 283.44 兆元，年增 4.8% ，前三季增加
11.52 兆元。
2   第三季末房地產貸款餘額 50.08 兆元，年減 5.2% ，前三季減少 1.90 兆元；其中房地產開發貸款
12.37 兆元、年減 9.1% ，個⼈房貸 35.91 兆元、年減 4.1% 。
3   普惠⼩微貸款餘額 38.50 兆元，年增 6.7% ，增速⽐各項貸款⾼ 1.9 個百分點，前三季增加 1.93 兆
元。
4   個⼈房貸前三季淨減 1.10 兆元，顯⽰居⺠仍在降低房貸負債，新增信⽤主要流向⼩微企業等政

策⽀持領域，⽽⾮房地產。
5   中共⼆⼗屆五中全會 10/29 閉幕，公報尚未公布；房地產與內需⽀持政策是否加碼，是信貸結
構能否改善的下⼀個觀察點（預期）。
三⼤結論
1   整體貸款年增 4.8% ⽽房地產貸款年減 5.2% ，證實信⽤沒有流向地產，與 8 ⽉新增貸款⾼於預
期但房地產到位資⾦年減的狀況⼀致；中國成⻑仍靠出⼝與⾼技術製造，內需修復未獲信貸數據
⽀持。
2   房地產開發貸款年減 9.1% ，代表建商融資收縮仍在加深， 1 ⾄ 9 ⽉房地產投資年減 20.6% 的趨
勢短期難以反轉；降準等寬鬆措施若只增加貸款總量，難以轉化為居⺠需求。
3   當個⼈房貸由減轉增、開發貸款降幅收斂且零售與⺠間投資同步回升時，才應提⾼中國股市總
體權重；在此之前，以出⼝型先進製造作衛星，內需與地產相關部位維持低配。
事件  10
10/28 渣打第三季稅前盈餘 23 億美元年增 29% ，上調全年營收指引

### Page 9

五⼤重點
1   渣打集團 10/28 公布第三季營業收⼊ 58 億美元，年增 14% ，稅前盈餘 23 億美元，年增 29% ，⾼
於市場預估 20.9 億美元；淨利 15.0 億美元，去年同期為 10.3 億美元。
2   有形股本報酬率（ RoTE ） 15.7% ，較去年同期上升 520 個基點；財富管理解決⽅案收⼊年增
26% ，全球市場業務收⼊年增 21% 。
3   公司將 2026 年營業收⼊成⻑指引上調⾄⾼於原訂的 5% ⾄ 7% （固定匯率、剔除重⼤項⽬），預
估全年 RoTE 約 14% ；當季已計⼊中東衝突相關淨信⽤成本 3,400 萬美元。
4   財報公布後，⾹港上市股價盤中⼀度⼤漲 8.7% ，倫敦股價同⽇漲逾 4% ，在富時 100 指數中表
現居前。
5   財務⻑表⽰，亞洲資料中⼼建置熱潮將帶來融資商機，但資料中⼼相關融資⽬前在資產負債表
中占⽐「並不⾼」，相關業務仍在擴充。
三⼤結論
1   渣打成⻑主要來⾃財富管理與市場交易等⾮利息收⼊，⽽⾮單靠利差，符合以資本充⾜、收⼊

多元作為⼤型⾦融機構選股條件的看法；⾼利率環境下，⼿續費收⼊多元的銀⾏較能抵銷存款成
本上升。
2   中東衝突相關信⽤成本僅 3,400 萬美元，顯⽰⽬前地緣衝擊對亞洲與新興市場銀⾏資產品質影
響有限；但能源價格若持續⾼檔，能源進⼝國企業與家庭的償債壓⼒仍是後續⾵險。
3   當亞洲資料中⼼融資與財富管理收⼊持續成⻑、信⽤成本維持低檔時，可在⾦融類股中提⾼收
⼊多元的⼤型銀⾏⽐重；若衝突擴⼤使信⽤成本上升或交易收⼊回落，應降低評價已反彈的銀⾏
部位。
待確認
1. 與上⼀輪⽐對結果：⽐對「全球⼗⼤財經事件｜ 2026/10/28 07:50 （台灣時間）」，本輪排除重複主題： SK 海⼒⼠
財報後續（含 10/28 KOSPI ⼤漲 5% 重回 6,000 點）；以⾊列⼤選（含 10/28 初步計票）；伊朗停戰條件後續（含伊朗拒
絕卡達協議草案、美國警告期中選舉後打擊伊朗）；俄烏和談後續（含三⽅會談延⾄ 11/4 後）；諮商會消費者信⼼、波
⾳、 5 年期公債標售、耐久財訂單、滙豐、台灣央⾏放款與投資均未重列； Meta 股權募資等更早已寫過的主題亦未重

列。
2. 事件 6 第 3 點 WTI 重回百元屬上⼀輪事件 3 「 WTI 跌破百元」同⼀議題的新⾏情（ 10/28 收盤，晚於上⼀輪資料同步時
間 10/28 07:50 ），本輪僅⼀句帶過，事件主體為天然氣突破 100 歐元（台灣時間 10/28 21:36 起）。
3. 72 ⼩時內央⾏利率調整：加拿⼤央⾏ 10/28 升息已單獨成件並列第 2 ；巴基斯坦央⾏ 10/26 升息 50 個基點⾄ 12.0% ，
⾮主要經濟體，未單獨成件；聯準會與紐⻄蘭央⾏（ 10/28 意外維持 2.75% ）未調整； ECB （ 10/29 ）、⽇銀（ 10/30 ）
決議尚未公布；中國⼈⾏僅進⾏例⾏ 7 天期逆回購，利率 1.40% 不變。

4. 單⼀來源或未能直接核對官⽅原⽂的數字：渣打財報數字取⾃財報快訊，未能讀取官⽅新聞稿；英國 OBR 與預算
案數字取⾃預算演說即時發布， OBR 官網未能讀取；聯準會聲明以兩份中⽂全⽂譯本交叉核對； TTF 價格為盤中觸
價，未取得官⽅結算價；美國貿易與庫存為先期值。

告年增 9% 由財報數字推算； 12 ⽉升息 1 碼機率在不同時點介於約 78% ⾄ 90% ，本報告採 10/29 06:06 的 78.4% 。
6. Alphabet 與微軟股價變動為盤後交易， 10/29 正式開盤反應待確認；美國對伊朗期中選舉後軍事⾏動的說法尚未獲
官⽅證實。
7. 九⼤分類中，台灣公司與台灣經濟與央⾏近 72 ⼩時無新的重⼤⼀⼿事件（台積電先進封裝漲價仍為傳聞， 10/28 台
股上漲 2% ⾄ 47,035 點未創新⾼）；中美國際政治經濟競爭無新的⼀⼿事件，機器⼈與⼯業機械公告適⽤所有國家，未

特定針對中國；歐洲、⽇本經濟與央⾏因 ECB 與⽇銀決議尚未公布⽽未⼊選；⽇本公司（⽇⽴上半財年營收年增
17.3% ）份量次要，未⼊選。
8. 六⼤實質內涵本期皆有涵蓋：重要經濟數據（事件 7 、 9 ）、政府與財政政策（事件 5 、 8 ）、央⾏官員發⾔（事件 1 、
2 ）、劇烈⾏情⽰警（事件 6 ）、⼤型公司財報（事件 3 、 4 、 10 ）、 AI 重要發展（事件 3 、 4 ）。

聯準會升息了，投等債還能買嗎？ 
 9 月聯準會升息一碼，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息，雖年內尚具升息空間，但
資金流仍持續流入美國投等債，顯示市況紛擾無損其資金避風港的特質。

出刊日：2026/10/8 
222 期

### Page 2

2026

02-8751-6665 
 www.fubon.com 
台北市大安區仁愛路四段 169 號 
固本收利

本期內容： 
⚫ 經濟數據、市場供
需等持續干擾債市 
⚫ 美國投等債仍為資
金良好避風港 
⚫ 建議逢利率回彈時
分批佈局投等債

重點摘要

■ 為因應頑固的通膨壓力 ，9 月聯準會升
息 1 碼，預期年內至少再升息 1 碼。
第 3 季美國投等債價格承壓是受美國
數據強勁、大量投等債發債、通膨預期
加上聯準會鷹派訊號等因素影響。

■ 總體經濟強韌提供絕佳的信用支撐，
高評級公司違約風險或信用降評機率
微乎其微。今年以來美國投資等級債
維持淨流入， 顯示市場動盪時美國投
等債仍為資金避風港。

■ 目前美國投等債殖利率 處於近年相對
較高的位置，投資人應以息收為主要
考量，建議投資人把握殖利率回彈時
機，分批進行佈局鎖定高債息收益。

▋ 聯準會 3 年多來首度升息 
9 月聯準會票委全數贊成通過升息 1 碼，將
聯邦資金利率目標調升為 3.75%至 4.00%
區間，為 2023 年 7 月以來首度升息。美國
經濟維持穩健擴張 ，聯準會對於 2026、
2027 年經濟成長預期均上修 0.1 個百分點
至 2.3%、2.4%，就業供需平穩且失業率穩
定，2026、2027 年失業率預期均下修 0.2
個百分點至 4.1%，會後聲明新增國內支出
具韌性描述。今年 PCE 和核心 PCE 均上修
0.1 個百分點至 3.7%和 3.4%，9 月聯準會
升息是為因應頑固的通膨壓力，18 名與會
官員中有 16 位委員預期今年內至少再升息
1 碼，明年聯準會維持 6 月的通膨預期， 政
策利率亦維持不變。(圖二、圖三)

▋ 殖利率飆壓抑 Q3 投等債價格 
第 3 季美國投等債價格下跌，是今年最差
單季表現 ， 相比去年第3 季以及過去 30 年
第 3 季的投等債價格表現亦相對遜色。這
是受到近期美國就業 、 生產數據表現強勁 ，
今年前 8 個月科技相關投等債發債量已超
越去年全年表現 ， 中東戰爭持續支撐國際油
價在高位 ， 而居高不下的通膨預期持續推升
美債殖利率走勢等 ， 加上聯準會主席Warsh
自 6 月主持個人首場利率會議起，即反覆
強調聯準會穩定物價的鷹派訊號， 持續為投
等債走勢構成壓力。(圖四、圖五)

▋ 那投資等級債還能買嗎？ 
美國經濟數據強勁意味在強韌的總體經濟
下 ， 提供絕佳的信用支撐 ， 即便面臨高利率
環境 ， 大型龍頭企業營運有望維持穩健 ， 加
上企業帳上現金充裕 ， 投資等級高的公司違
約風險或信用降評的機率將微乎其微。

隨著各國債券殖利率攀升 ，加劇全球風險資
產高檔震盪 ， 市場避險情緒明顯升溫， 今年
以來美國投資等級債維持淨流入， 根據小摩
數據顯示，至 9/24 流入投等債資金已直逼
2025 年，相比非投等債和新興市場債僅達
去年約一半的水準，顯示在市場動盪時 ， 美
國投資等級債的高流動性和 極佳避險韌性
仍為資金提供避風港而持續吸金。(圖六)

圖六：目前流入投等債資金直逼去年水準 
資料來源：JPM，2026/9/24

▋ 殖利率達近年高點具吸引力 
目前美國投等債利差落在近年低點 ， 相對較
無收斂空間 ， 但殖利率則落在5%至 6%間，
處於近年相對較高的位置 ， 高殖利具有投資
吸引力 ， 投資人應以息收為主要考量 ， 目前
包含金融公司、通訊產業債殖利率在6%以
上 ， 為殖利率相對領先的產業族群 ， 建議在
當前升息預期濃厚下 ， 投資人應把握殖利率
回彈的時機 ， 分批進行佈局以鎖定高債息收
益。(圖七、圖八)

从继续实施好适度宽松的货币政策、进⼀步加强和完善⾦融监管、提升⾦融服务实体经济适配

场 “ 科技板 ” 累计发⾏科技创新债券 3.3 万亿元。

## Document 694: 宏观月报：风险过后，关注机会

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观月报：风险过后，关注机会.pdf
Report source: 华泰期货
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

宏观月报 | 2026-11-01

风险过后，关注机会 
策略摘要 
回顾10月，市场的波动率如期回升。从流动性的角度，美联储9月的加息以及10月的鹰派都增加了市场的压力，驱
动美国长端利率从持续上行转向了阶段性的调整。而国内的宏观政策依然是结构性的，对于 “新经济”的倾斜和对
于“旧经济”的稳定依然是这里的主线。

展望11月，随着美国“中期选举”这一不确定事件的落地，尽管在明年1月国会正式履职前仍存在时间上的空档，但
是我们最大的不确定“投票”已经从象征意义上过去，市场对于负面的定价预期将有所改善。相比较当前市场的持
续压力，我们认为11月可能是关注市场拐点的重要时间窗口之一。

市场分析 
宏观周期：市场波动，数据回落。中美两大经济引擎从数据层面正在经历短期熄火的压力。从美国来看，尽管PMI
仍显示出环比的扩张， 但驱动生产力的核心面临短期的压力， 其中Alphabet三季度自由现金流意外转负提升了市场
对于行业稳定性的担忧。 从中国的角度来看，10月PMI环比的放缓结束了景气指数稳定的区间， 增加了市场对于经
济的下行担忧，存量政策的进一步积极甚至增量政策的预期也存在上升空间。

价格周期：实物上行，资产调整。一方面，10月在地缘压力上升的状态下油价继续维持在相对高位，推动同比意
义上的通胀压力短期内仍难消退——10月PPI同比增长美国+6.3%， 中国+5.0%——即使地缘在11月缓和， 基数效应
下的高通胀短期内仍将延续。另一方面，10月资产价格表现出更大幅度的压力，从新兴市场到发达经济体，从科
技板块到整体权益资产。这种价格层面的分化将驱动全球财富进入到再次分配。

政策周期：等待政策的扳机落地。10月尽管中国央行将利率锚从存贷款转向隔夜逆回购，新增对于债市的流动性
补充政策工具，但是总体仍表现出结构性强于总量性特征。而美联储在10月份的政策态度转变更值得我们关注：
一方面在对经济判断转向“强劲”的同时政策函数却进一步向“稳增长”倾斜， 尽管“控通胀”没有改变加息的方向， 但
是在资产价格存在压力的背景下政策边际上出现了缓和，政策的节奏出现了调整；另一方面，美联储也后置了“资
产负债表工作组”给出结论的时间，为市场的流动性环境提供短期的暖意（见美联储系列三十六） 。

策略 
战略上，我们继续维持宏观策略8月份开始的向防守切换。从8月底美联储央行年会上释放收紧信号到9月美联储如
期加息，印证了我们这一判断。宏观资产层面，继续战略性维持国债的高配和股指的低配，商品保持中性配置。

战术上，我们将短期的观点从“中性”上调至“积极”。美联储流动性收紧节奏的放缓（10.29） 、美国中期选举不确定
性的落地（11.3）以及中美自八点共识（9.24）后紧邻的APEC会晤（11.18）和G20会晤（12.14） ，市场的风险偏好
在短期的压力后或将迎来转变，我们认为值得关注美国中期选举投票后的市场机会。 
风险 
地缘风险超预期升级，中美会晤低预期

一、经济状态：中国景气指数环比放缓

华泰宏观热力跟踪显示景气状况短期出现了一定的调整（受中国10月制造业PMI影响：49.3） ，需要关注中国以
外地区的变动情况（10月综合PMI初值：美国57.6，欧元区51.9，日本52.3，英国53.1） 。最新数据显示，尽管9
月消费、 出口和生产仍保持在扩张的第一象限， 但受到中国PMI数据点的影响， 景气度已经降至收缩的第三象限。

全球10月实物型通胀表现分化：亚洲的越南、菲律宾、中国、韩国，欧洲的意大利、法国以及美洲的美国等保
持暖意，但是南美的墨西哥、欧洲的德国、亚洲的越南、贸易型的新加坡等仍趋冷状态。同时10月份全球资产
价格出现了较大的回落， 波动率有所放大。 前者约束了美国转向“降息”， 但是后者为美联储的政策转向提供了“加
分项”。
